## 6.S Smoke validation on ≥10 JCA-226 APKs (round-8 — gates A/B/C; Coverage.aj gate DROPPED)

**Goal**: verify the fourteen round-8 closures (§4.{W,O,R,N,V,X,TT,AT,Y,T,B,D,I,E}) produce correct monitor events on real APKs from the JCA-226 dataset, with §4.E `execution(...)` validated via grammar-tests fixture + dexdump per U-decision (no current JCA-226 APK uses `execution()` positively, so positive-evidence is sourced from the fixture path). The round-7 Coverage.aj end-to-end gate (Gate D) is DROPPED — Coverage.aj is absorbed by `coverage-weaver`, which already produced the production coverage data in experimento-20260508.

- [ ] 6.S.1 Pick ≥10 APKs from the JCA-226 instrumentable subset (re-using INV-INS-31 baseline). Selection MUST cover the fourteen closures collectively (one APK per closure pattern; §4.E is exercised via a synthetic fixture in grammar-tests rather than a real APK, since no JCA spec uses `execution(...)` positively today). At least one APK MUST have nested try-catch DEX topology (for §4.T).
- [ ] 6.S.2 Pre-change snapshot: re-instrument ≥10 APKs with `instr-cli.jar` from `HEAD~N` (pre-closure); run on emulator under standard JCA MOP spec; capture monitor event stream + per-closure pattern-match counts.
- [ ] 6.S.3 Post-change snapshot: re-instrument with `HEAD` (post-closure); run; capture.
- [ ] 6.S.4 Three gates (round-8 — drops round-7's Gate D; U-decision adds §4.E fixture/bytecode gate):
  - **GATE A (hard, no-new-VerifyError)**: post-change DEX MUST pass ART install + DexBackedDexFile round-trip on all ≥10 APKs. Any new VerifyError REVERTS the responsible closure series.
  - **GATE B (hard, monotonic non-decrease event count)**: total post-change event count ≥ pre-change. Round-8 note: §4.I `if(...)` runtime delegation can short-circuit events (when the guard returns false); the gate is monotonic OVER THE SET OF APKS WHERE NO `if(...)` GUARD IS EXERCISED. The smoke harness MUST distinguish: APKs exercising `if(...)` clauses are evaluated against the closure-specific positive-evidence gate (Gate C); APKs not exercising them are evaluated against the monotonic gate.
  - **GATE C (hard, positive-evidence per closure)**: each of the fourteen closures MUST produce ≥1 new event in ≥1 APK whose pattern matches the closure. **U-decision §4.E validation split (2026-05-28 per Codex meta-review)**: because no current JCA-226 APK exercises `execution(...)` positively (every current consumer is absorbed upstream), §4.E's Gate C evidence cannot come from a real APK. Instead, §4.E SHALL be validated by TWO orthogonal artefacts that together carry the same weight as a real-APK gate: (i) `ExecutionPointcutGrammarTest.executionUniversalMatchesEveryMethod` AND `.executionByOwnerName` AND `.executionWithTPlusOwner` AND `.executionEmitsAtEntryForBeforeAdvice` AND `.executionEmitsAtAllReturnPathsForAfterAdvice` AND `.executionDualInstrumentationNoDouble` — six grammar-level fixture tests asserting matcher + emitter correctness against synthetic class fixtures; AND (ii) `ExecutionPointcutBytecodeTest.weavenDexHasInvokeStaticAtMethodEntry` AND `.weavenDexHasInvokeStaticBeforeEachReturn` — bytecode-level inspection of the woven DEX (using dexlib2 to walk the instruction list of the weaver-output method) asserting the `invoke-static` to the advice helper appears at the expected offsets. A closure producing zero new events on any other (non-§4.E) APK is either inert (bug) or the APK selection was wrong.
- [ ] 6.S.5 If any gate fails, REVERT the offending closure commit(s) (bisect-friendly per atomic commits) and re-evaluate.
- [ ] 6.S.6 Commit: `chore(gh62): ≥10-APK smoke validation PASS for round-8 closures (gates A/B/C)` with `refs #62`. Push.

# Deferred-by-Design — gh62-aspectj-grammar-coverage (round-8)

**Purpose**: snapshot of every AspectJ construction the gh62 round-8 closure series explicitly does NOT implement, with the demand evidence (zero at the pipeline stage that would consume it) and rationale per entry. Matches one-to-one with matrix rows whose `Verdict ∈ {EXPLICIT-NO-OP, NOT-NEEDED}`.

**Frozen-snapshot tripwire**: SHA-256 of this file is committed to `rvsec-android/rvsec-instrumentation-dexlib2/grammar-tests/src/test/resources/deferred.snapshot.sha256` per design D7 (round-7 reformulation). `MatrixIntegrityTest.testDeferredDocumentIsFrozenPostArchive` reads both files and fails the build if they diverge (positive enforcement of the "frozen post-archive" property). Round-8 fix: the snapshot is generated in the same commit as the final `deferred.md` edit (see tasks §1.4), eliminating the round-7 race condition flagged by cross-LLM review.

**Round-8 redesign — absorption-aware demand**: round-7 framed scope by **source-level `.mop`/`.aj` demand**. Round-8 reframes scope by **pipeline-level demand** — the demand that actually reaches the dexlib2 instrumenter after the upstream stages (JavaMOP compiler, AJC, `coverage-weaver`) have absorbed everything they can absorb. The Three Empirical Audits Appendix at the bottom of this file documents the evidence base.

Concretely: a `.mop` source may declare 64 `condition(...)` clauses in `jca/`, but the `.aj` produced by the JavaMOP compiler contains **zero** — the condition logic has been folded into the corresponding `*RuntimeMonitor.*Event(...)` method body that the dexlib2 instrumenter already invokes. The round-7 closure §4.G (`ConditionGuardEmitter`, ~80 LOC) was attacking a construction the instrumenter never sees. Round-8 catches seven such closures.

**What is NOT here**: every construction with `DemandCounter ≥ 1` at the pipeline stage that consumes it ships in-change per design D9 (round-8 demand-driven absorption, post round-7 absorption-aware refinement). The round-6 `Fix-now`/`Follow-up` ledger buckets remain eliminated. AspectJ extensions outside the closed enumeration (`lock`/`unlock` per OQ3; per-clauses, ITDs, `declare warning/error/soft`, annotation-style aspects, generics in type patterns per OQ4) are NOT in the matrix and NOT here — they are AUSENTE-JUSTIFICADO in design.md.

---

## 1. Deferred-by-design (EXPLICIT-NO-OP)

Production code raises `UnsupportedOperationException` AND a passing test asserts the throw. The matrix row's `Evidence` column cites both the test FQN AND the `file:line` of the no-op declaration. If a future MOP spec uses one of these constructions, the matrix row's `EXPLICIT-NO-OP` verdict will fail loudly via the UOE assertion test, signalling the need to open a sub-change implementing the closure.

| AspectJ syntax | Production no-op anchor | Assertion test FQN | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `around` advice | `EmitterDispatch.java:61-65` — `throw new UnsupportedOperationException("around advice not supported")` | `EmitterDispatchTest.java:54-59` — `aroundAdviceRejected` asserts `UnsupportedOperationException` | Zero demand across all four corpora (`DemandCounter` returns 0/0/0/0). Implementing `around` requires wrapper-method synthesis: the emitter creates a synthetic method that calls the advice, and the advice calls `proceed()` which invokes the original — a substantial DEX-level feature with no current use case. |
| `proceed(...)` keyword in around body | Same `EmitterDispatch.java:61-65` — `around` rejection covers `proceed` by reflection (the around emit never runs, so no separate code path exists for `proceed`) | Same `EmitterDispatchTest.java:54-59` | Subsumed by `around` deferral. The matrix row cites the same anchor; the EXPLICIT-NO-OP verdict applies by reflection. |

---

## 2. Deferred-by-design (NOT-NEEDED)

`DemandCounter` returns zero at the pipeline stage that would consume the construction. There are two paths under this verdict and the matrix `Evidence` column distinguishes them:

- **Path α** — zero source-level demand AND no implementation. The construction does not appear in any of the four corpora at any stage.
- **Path β** — non-zero source-level demand in `.mop` and/or `.aj`, but absorbed by an upstream pipeline stage (JavaMOP compiler, `coverage-weaver`, AJC weaver, `MonitorRuntime` dispatch loop) before reaching the dexlib2 pipeline. Pipeline-stage demand at dexlib2 is zero.

For both paths, the Evidence column cites an enabled passing test asserting the absence of pipeline demand. For path β, the test additionally asserts that the upstream-absorbed equivalent input is produced (or absorbed equivalently) regardless of upstream source syntax. If a future corpus introduces non-zero **pipeline** demand for any deferred row, `MatrixIntegrityTest.testPipelineDemandCountsReproducible` fails the build (the matrix's pipeline-demand cell will diverge from the helper's output), forcing amendment via a new sub-change.

### 2.1 NOT-NEEDED path α (zero source demand + no implementation)

| AspectJ syntax | Anchor | Assertion test FQN | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `get(FieldPattern)` field-access pointcut | `MISSING` in parser/matcher/emitter | `FieldAccessGrammarTest.getFieldAccessHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. Implementation would require a new join-point family (iput/sget/iput-object/sget-object DEX opcodes). The earlier draft's 356/158 count was a substring grep conflating `call(* Foo.get(...))` method-name calls with field-access pointcuts; corrected to zero. |
| `set(FieldPattern)` field-access pointcut | `MISSING` in parser/matcher/emitter | `FieldAccessGrammarTest.setFieldAccessHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same as `get(FieldPattern)`. |
| `this(name)` value-binding | `MISSING` in parser/matcher (no `ThisPC` class) | `ThisGrammarTest.thisBindingHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. The dexlib2 binding-form `target(name)` covers the common receiver-binding case; `this()` distinguishes the bound aspect instance from the join-point receiver, which the corpora do not exercise. |
| `this(Type)` type-matching | `MISSING` in parser/matcher | `ThisGrammarTest.thisTypeMatchingHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same as `this(name)`. |
| `withincode(...)` | `MISSING` in parser; falls into `NamedRefPC` always-match in matcher | `WithincodeGrammarTest.withincodeHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. Would filter join points by enclosing method signature — niche use case. |
| `cflow(...)` | `MISSING` in parser; `NamedRefPC` always-match in matcher | `CflowGrammarTest.cflowHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. Runtime flow tracking requires a thread-local stack of active frames and instrumentation in every method on the candidate flow — substantial runtime cost for zero current use. |
| `cflowbelow(...)` | Same as `cflow(...)` | `CflowGrammarTest.cflowBelowHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same rationale as `cflow(...)`. |
| `initialization(...)` | `MISSING` in parser; `NamedRefPC` always-match | `InitializationGrammarTest.initializationHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. `staticinitialization(...)` is in-change via §4.Y; constructor-related variants have zero demand. |
| `preinitialization(...)` | Same as `initialization(...)` | `InitializationGrammarTest.preInitializationHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same as `initialization(...)`. |

| `@within(...)` AspectJ 5 | Same parser-crash | `AnnotationPointcutGrammarTest.atWithinHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same. |
| `@withincode(...)` AspectJ 5 | Same parser-crash | `AnnotationPointcutGrammarTest.atWithincodeHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same. |
| Standalone `target(name)` / `args(name)` without `&&`-composition | Routes through `PointcutMatcher.java:106-108` for `Match.empty(pe)` resolution | `CompositionGrammarTest.standaloneTargetArgsHasZeroCorpusDemand` | All corpus uses are `&&`-composed with `call()`; standalone matching is not exercised. |
| `!cflow` parser specialization | `MISSING` in parser | `CompositionGrammarTest.negativeCflowHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. Only `!within`/`!target`/`!args` negations appear in corpora. |
| `!cflowbelow` parser specialization | `MISSING` in parser | `CompositionGrammarTest.negativeCflowBelowHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same. |
| `!if` parser specialization | `MISSING` in parser | `CompositionGrammarTest.negativeIfHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same. |
| `!handler` parser specialization | `MISSING` in parser | `CompositionGrammarTest.negativeHandlerHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Same. |
| `T+` inside `!within(...)` | `matchesTypePattern` strips `+` before exact-match | `TypePatternGrammarTest.tPlusInsideNegativeWithinHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. Positive `within(T+)` is also deferred in round-8 (Coverage.aj-only consumer; Coverage.aj absorbed by `coverage-weaver` — see §2.2-D below); the negative form has no use. |
| SignaturePattern modifier `!public` | `MISSING` in parser (positive visibility is stripped silently) | `SignatureModifierGrammarTest.negatedVisibilityHasZeroCorpusDemand` | Zero demand. |

Round-8 reclassification 2026-05-28 (P-decision per cross-LLM meta-review): moved from §2.2.2 path β to §2.1 path α because source demand is zero across all four corpora — path β semantics requires `SourceDemand ≥ 1` AND an upstream absorber; handler() has neither. The earlier "absorbed by NamedRefPC family-of-pointcuts decision" framing conflated "no consumer exists" with "an upstream stage absorbs the consumer". | `HandlerGrammarTest.handlerAbsorbedByNamedRefPC` (renamed `handlerHasZeroCorpusDemand` after the reclassification — see tasks §1.3) | Zero source demand. No DEX-level analogue for exception-handler join points. |
| `declare precedence: A, B;` aspect-ordering | `MISSING` in parser.
